}\n}\n\n/// Errors that might occur while loading the configuration.\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum ConfigLoadError {\n    #[error(\"config file parsing failed: {0}\")]\n    Parse(ConfigError),\n        )\n            .bind(tenant_id)\n            .bind(offset)\n            .bind(per_page)\n            .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n            .await?;\n\n            let total: (i64,) =     .bind(tenant_id)\n            .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n            .await?;\n\n            (rows, total.0)\n        };\n\n        Ok((entries, total as u64))\n    }\n}\n\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n//  SERVICE LAYER /////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n\npub struct LedgerEntryQuerySvc<R> {\n    repo: Arc<R>,\n    redis: RedisPool,\n}\n\nimpl<R> LedgerEntryQuerySvc<R>\nwhere\n    R: LedgerEntryRepository + 'static,\n{\n    pub fn new(repo: Arc<R>, redis: RedisPool) -> Self {\n        Self { repo, redis }\n    }\n\n    fn cache_key(tenant: Uuid, query: &ListQuery) -> String {\n        let filter = query.account_code.as_deref().unwrap_or(\"_\");\n        let page = query.page.unwrap_or(1);\n        let per_page = query.per_page.unwrap_or(50);\n        format!(\"ledger_entries:{tenant}:{filter}:{page}:{per_page}\")\n    }\n\n    #[instrument(skip(self))]\n    pub async fn list(\n        &self,\n        tenant: Uuid,\n        query: ListQuery,\n    ) -> Result<Paginated<LedgerEntryDto>, ServiceError> {\n        // 1. Try cache (30ms timeout to avoid head-of-line blocking)\n        let key = Self::cache_key(tenant, &query);\n        if let Ok(Some(raw)) = self.try_fetch_cache(&key).await {\n            debug!(%tenant, %key, \"Cache hit\");\n            return serde_json::from_slice::<Paginated<LedgerEntryDto>>(&raw)\n                .map_err(ServiceError::CacheSerialization);\n        }\n\n        // 2. Query repository\n        debug!(%tenant, \"Cache miss – querying DB\");\n        let (entries, total) = self.repo.list(tenant, &query).await?;\n        let dto: Vec<LedgerEntryDto> = entries.into_iter().map(Into::into).collect();\n\n        let page = query.page.unwrap_or(1);\n        let per_page = query.per_page.unwrap_or(50);\n        let total_pages = ((total as f64) / (per_page as f64)).ceil() as u32;\n\n        let payload = Paginated {\n            data: dto,\n            page,\n            per_page,\n            total_items: total,\n            total_pages,\n        };\n\n        // 3. Backfill cache (best-effort)\n        let _ = self.persist_cache(&key, &payload).await;\n\n        Ok(payload)\n    }\n\n    async fn try_fetch_cache(&self, key: &str) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, ServiceError> {\n        let mut conn: RedisConn = self.redis.get().await?;\n        // short timeout to protect against redis hiccups\n        let result: Option<Vec<u8>> =\n            timeout(Duration::from_millis(30), conn.get(key)).await??;\n        Ok(result)\n    }\n\n    async fn persist_cache<T: Serialize>(&self, key: &str, value: &T) -> Result<(), ServiceError> {\n        let mut conn: RedisConn = self.redis.get().await?;\n        let data = serde_json::to_vec(value)?;\n        let _: () = conn\n            .set_ex(key, data, 60) // 60 seconds TTL\n            .await\n            .map_err(ServiceError::Cache)?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum ServiceError {\n    #[error(\"repository error: {0}\")]\n    Repo(#[from] RepositoryError),\n\n    #[error(\"redis error: {0}\")]\n    Cache(#[from] deadpool_redis::redis::RedisError),\n\n    #[error(\"serialization error: {0}\")]\n    CacheSerialization(#[from] serde_json::Error),\n}\n\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n//  API HANDLER ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n\n#[instrument(skip_all, fields(tenant=%auth.tenant_id, user=%auth.user_id))]\npub async fn list_ledger_entries_handler<R>(\n    auth: JwtAuth,\n    Extension(svc): Extension<Arc<LedgerEntryQuerySvc<R>>>,\n    headers: HeaderMap,\n    Query(mut query): Query<ListQuery>,\n) -> Result<Response, ApiError>\nwhere\n    R: LedgerEntryRepository + 'static,\n{\n    // Merge defaults\n    if query.page.is_none() {\n        query.page = Some(1);\n    }\n    if query.per_page.is_none() {\n        query.per_page = Some(50);\n    }\n\n    // Validate\n    query\n        .validate()\n        .map_err(|e| mut resp = json.into_response();\n    {\n        let headers = resp.headers_mut();\n        headers.insert(\"x-total-items\", result.total_items.into());\n        headers.insert(\"x-total-pages\", result.total_pages.into());\n    }\n    Ok(resp)\n}\n\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n//  API ROUTER FACTORY }\n}\n\nimpl From<ServiceError> for ApiError {\n    fn from(e: ServiceError) -> Self {\n        match e {\n            ServiceError::Repo(repo_err) => {\n                error!(error = %repo_err, &NaiveDate) -> Result<(), ValidationError> {\n    // Actual inter-field validation happens in `normalize()`.\n    Ok(())\n}\n\nimpl LedgerEntryQuery {\n    /// Normalizes unset pagination parameters and performs cross-field validation.\n    pub fn normalize(mut self) -> Result<Self, ServiceError> {\n        self.page_size.get_or_insert(DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE);\n        self.page.get_or_insert(1);\n\n        // Ensure `from_date` comes before `to_date`.\n        if let (Some(from), Some(to)) = (self.from_date, self.to_date) {\n            if from > to {\n                return Err(ServiceError::Validation(\n                    \"from_date must not be after to_date\".into(),\n                ));\n            }\n        }\n        Ok(self)\n    }\n\n    /// Calculates SQL OFFSET based on (page, page_size).\n    pub fn offset(&self) -> u32 {\n        (self.page.unwrap_or(1) - 1) * self.page_size.unwrap_or(DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE)\n    }\n\n    /// Calculates SQL LIMIT.\n    pub fn limit(&self) -> u32 {\n        self.page_size.unwrap_or(DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE)\n    }\n}\n\n/* ---------- Paginated envelope  ---------- */\n\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]\npub struct Paginated<T> conn = self.client.get_async_connection().await?;\n        let serialized = serde_json::to_string(payload)\n            .map_err(|e| ServiceError::Unknown(e.to_string()))?;\n        conn.set_ex(cache_key, serialized, CACHE_TTL_SECONDS)\n            .await\n            .map_err(ServiceError::Cache)\n    }\n}\n\n/* ---------- Service layer  ---------- */\n\npub struct LedgerEntryService<R: LedgerEntryRepository, C: CacheProvider> {\n    repo:  R,\n    cache: C,\n}\n\nimpl<R: LedgerEntryRepository, C: CacheProvider> LedgerEntryService<R, C> {\n    pub fn new(repo: R, cache: C) -> Self {\n        Self { repo, cache }\n    }\n\n    /// Returns a paginated collection, using Redis as read-through cache.\n    #[instrument(skip(self))]\n    pub async fn get_paginated(\n        &self,\n        raw_query: LedgerEntryQuery,\n    ) -> Result<Paginated<LedgerEntryView>, ServiceError> {\n        // (1) Validate & normalize\n        let query = raw_query\n            .validate()\n            .map_err(|e| ServiceError::Validation(e.to_string()))?\n            .normalize()?;\n\n        // (2) Compute cache key\n        let cache_key = {\n            let mut hasher = Sha256::new();\n            hasher.update(b\"ledger_entries:\");\n            hasher.update(serde_json::to_vec(&query).unwrap());\n            format!(\"{:x}\", hasher.finalize())\n        };\n\n        // (3) Attempt cache hit\n        if let Some(page) = self.cache.get_cached_page(&cache_key).await? {\n            debug!(\"cache hit for {}\", cache_key);\n            return Ok(page);\n        }\n\n        debug!(\"cache miss for {}\", cache_key);\n\n        // (4) Fetch from repository\n        let (items, total) = self.repo.fetch_page(&query).await?;\n\n        let page = Paginated {\n            items,\n            page: query.page.unwrap(),\n            page_size: query.page_size.unwrap(),\n            total,\n            has_next: (query.offset() as u64 + query.page_size.unwrap() as u64) < total,\n        };\n\n        // (5) Store in cache (fire-and-forget; don't hold client hostage)\n        let cache = self.cache.cache_page(&cache_key, &page);\n        tokio::spawn(async move {\n            if let Err(e) = cache.await {\n                error!(\"Failed to cache page: {:?}\", e);\n            }\n        });\n\n        Ok(page)\n    }\n}\n\n/*  345→\n   346→#[derive(Debug, Error)]\n   347→pub enum ValidationError {\n   348→    #[error(\"invalid pagination page (must be > 0)\")]\n   349→    InvalidPage,\n   350→    #[error(\"invalid page LedgerLink Nexus – Pagination + Response Caching + Repository glue\n//! ------------------------------------------------------------------\n//! This module brings together three core cross-cutting concerns that are heavily\n//! reused throughout the micro-service:\n//!   1. Generic, typed pagination helpers with validation\n//!   2. A small opinionated Redis-backed response-caching façade\n//!   3. A sample Postgres repository implementation for Ledger Entries\n//!\n//! It is **self-contained** so it can be pulled into integration/unit tests\n//! without touching the rest of the codebase.\n\n#![allow(clippy::module_inception)] // we intentionally group sub-modules here\n#![deny(rust_2018_idioms)]\n\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse futures::{future::BoxFuture, Future, FutureExt};\nuse serde::{de::DeserializeOwned, Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse sha2::{Digest, Sha256};\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::{select, time::timeout};\n\n//\n// ─────────────────────────────  ERRORS  ────────────────────────────────\n//\n\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum ApiError {\n    #[error(\"validation error: {0}\")]\n    Validation(String),\n\n    #[error(\"repository error: {0}\")]\n    Repository(String),\n\n    #[error(\"cache error: {0}\")]\n    Cache(String),\n\n    #[error(\"timeout after {0:?}\")]\n    Timeout(Duration),\n\n    #[error(transparent)]\n    Other(#[from] anyhow::Error),\n}\n\n//\n// ────────────────────────  PAGINATION PRIMITIVES  ──────────────────────\n//\nmod pagination {\n    use super::ApiError;\n    use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\n\n    /// Maximum number of items that can be requested in a single page\n    const MAX_PAGE_SIZE: u32 = 250;\n\n    /// Input query params coming from the client (View)\n    #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n    pub struct PageRequest {\n        pub page: u32,\n        pub size: u32,\n    }\n\n    impl Default for PageRequest {\n        fn default() -> Self {\n            Self { page: 1, size: 50 }\n        }\n    }\n\n    impl PageRequest {\n        /// Validates & bounds the request to ensure we never allocate unbounded\n        /// memory or inadvertently DoS ourselves.\n        pub fn validate(self) -> Result<Self, ApiError> {\n            if self.page == 0 {\n                return Err(ApiError::Validation(\"page index starts at 1\".into()));\n            }\n            if self.size == 0 || self.size > MAX_PAGE_SIZE {\n                return Err(ApiError::Validation(format!(\n                    \"size must be 1..={MAX_PAGE_SIZE}\"\n                )));\n            }\n            Ok(self)\n        }\n\n        /// Calculates SQL `OFFSET`\n        pub fn offset(self) -> u32 {\n            (self.page.saturating_sub(1)) * self.size\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Metadata returned alongside every paginated response\n    #[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n    pub struct PageMeta {\n        pub page: u32,\n        pub size: u32,\n        pub total: u64,\n        pub has_next: bool,\n    }\n\n    /// Wrapper DTO sent over the wire\n    #[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n    pub struct Paginated<T> {\n        pub data: Vec<T>,\n        pub meta: PageMeta,\n    }\n\n    impl<T> Paginated<T> {\n        pub fn new(data: Vec<T>, meta: PageMeta) -> Self {\n            Self { data, meta }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\npub use pagination::*;\n\n//\n// ─────────────────────────  REDIS RESPONSE CACHE  ──────────────────────\n//\nmod cache {\n    use super::{ApiError, Digest, Sha256};\n    use redis::{aio::ConnectionManager, AsyncCommands};\n    use serde::{de::DeserializeOwned, Serialize};\n    use std::time::Duration;\n\n    #[derive(Clone)]\n    pub struct ResponseCache {\n        conn: ConnectionManager,\n        ttl: Duration,\n    }\n\n    impl ResponseCache {\n        pub fn new(conn: ConnectionManager, ttl: Duration) -> Self {\n            Self { conn, ttl }\n        }\n\n        /// Hashes the *logical* cache key into a deterministic 64-char SHA-256\n        /// hexadecimal string that is safe for Redis.\n        fn hash_key(key: &str) -> String {\n            let mut hasher = Sha256::new();\n            hasher.update(key.as_bytes());\n            format!(\"{:x}\", hasher.finalize())\n        }\n\n        /// Fetches the cached value if present or, otherwise, executes the\n        /// async supplier closure, stores, and returns its result.\n        pub async fn get_or_insert_with<T, F, Fut>(\n            &mut self,\n            raw_key: &str,\n            supplier: F,\n        ) -> Result<T, ApiError>\n        where\n            T: Serialize + DeserializeOwned + Send + Sync,\n            F: FnOnce() -> Fut + Send,\n            Fut: std::future::Future<Output = Result<T, ApiError>> + Send,\n        {\n            let key = Self::hash_key(raw_key);\n\n            // 1) Try get\n            if let Ok(Some(buf)) = self.conn.get::<_, Vec<u8>>(&key).await {\n                // Do not treat JSON parsing errors as fatal – just invalidate that entry\n                if let Ok(value) = serde_json::from_slice::<T>(&buf) {\n                    return Ok(value);\n                } else {\n                    let _: Result<(), _> = self.conn.del(&key).await;\n                }\n            }\n\n            // 2) Miss: compute\n            let value = supplier().await?;\n\n            // 3) Store\n            let buf = serde_json::to_vec(&value).map_err(|e| ApiError::Cache(e.to_string()))?;\n            let _: () = self\n                .conn\n                .set_ex(&key, buf, self.ttl.as_secs() as usize)\n                .await\n                .map_err(|e| ApiError::Cache(e.to_string()))?;\n\n            Ok(value)\n        }\n\n        /// Helper to eagerly invalidate a known key (e.g. after a mutation).\n        pub async fn purge(&mut self, raw_key: &str) -> Result<(), ApiError> {\n            let key = Self::hash_key(raw_key);\n            self.conn\n                .del(&key)\n                .await\n                .map_err(|e| ApiError::Cache(e.to_string()))\n        }\n    }\n}\n\npub use cache::ResponseCache;\n\n//\n// ─────────────────────  DOMAIN DTO + REPOSITORY LAYER  ─────────────────\n//\nmod ledger {\n    use super::{pagination::PageRequest, ApiError};\n    use async_trait::async_trait;\n    use chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\n    use uuid::Uuid,\n        pub tenant_id: uuid::Uuid,\n        pub booked_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n        pub description: String,\n        pub debit: f64,\n        pub credit: f64,\n        pub currency: String,\n        pub created_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n        pub updated_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    }\n\n    /// Query- side repository\n    #[async_trait]\n    pub trait LedgerEntryRepository: Send + Sync + 'static {\n        async fn fetch_page(\n            &self,\n            tenant: uuid::Uuid,\n            req: PageRequest,\n        ) -> Result<(Vec<LedgerEntryDto>, u64), ApiError>;\n    }\n\n    /// Postgres implementation using `sqlx`\n    ///\n    /// NOTE: The actual SQL schema is simplified for brevity.\n    pub struct PgLedgerEntryRepository {\n        pool: sqlx::PgPool,\n    }\n\n    impl PgLedgerEntryRepository {\n        pub fn new(pool: sqlx::PgPool) -> Self {\n            Self { pool }\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[async_trait]\n    impl LedgerEntryRepository for PgLedgerEntryRepository {\n        async fn fetch_page(\n            &self,\n            tenant: uuid::Uuid,\n            req: PageRequest,\n        ) -> Result<(Vec<LedgerEntryDto>, u64), ApiError> {\n            let req = req.validate()?;\n\n            let rows: Vec<LedgerEntryDto> = sqlx::query_as!(\n                LedgerEntryDto,\n                r#\"\n                SELECT\n                    id,\n                    tenant_id,\n                    booked_at,\n                    description,\n                    debit,\n                    credit,\n                    currency,\n                    created_at,\n                    updated_at\n                FROM ledger_entries\n                WHERE tenant_id = $1\n                ORDER BY booked_at DESC\n                LIMIT $2\n                OFFSET $3\n                \"#,\n                tenant,\n                req.size as i64,\n                req.offset() as i64\n            )\n            .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n            .await\n            .map_err(|e| ApiError::Repository(e.to_string()))?;\n\n            // Count is stored in a materialized view or computed on demand\n            let total: (i64,) = sqlx::query_as(\n                r#\"SELECT COUNT(*) as count FROM ledger_entries WHERE tenant_id = $1\"#,\n            )\n            .bind(tenant)\n            .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n            .await\n            .map_err(|e| ApiError::Repository(e.to_string()))?;\n\n            Ok((rows, total.0 as u64))\n        }\n    }\n}\n\npub use ledger::{LedgerEntryDto, LedgerEntryRepository, PgLedgerEntryRepository};\n\n//\n// ─────────────────────────────  SERVICE LAYER  ─────────────────────────\n//\nmod service {\n    use super::{\n        cache::ResponseCache,\n        ledger::LedgerEntryRepository,\n        pagination::{PageMeta, PageRequest, Paginated},\n        ApiError,\n    };\n    use async_trait::async_trait;\n    use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};\n    use uuid::Uuid;\n\n    #[async_trait]\n    pub trait LedgerEntryService: Send + Sync + 'static {\n        async fn list_entries(\n            &self,\n            tenant: Uuid,\n            req: PageRequest,\n        ) -> Result<Paginated<super::LedgerEntryDto>, ApiError>;\n    }\n\n    pub struct LedgerEntryServiceImpl<R> {\n        repo: R,\n        cache: tokio::sync::Mutex<ResponseCache>, // Mutex to serialize Redis connection usage\n    }\n\n    impl<R> LedgerEntryServiceImpl<R> {\n        pub fn new(repo: R, cache: ResponseCache) -> Self {\n            Self {\n                repo,\n                cache: tokio::sync::Mutex::new(cache),\n            }\n        }\n\n        /// Builds a stable cache key based on tenant + pagination request.\n        fn make_cache_key(tenant: Uuid, req: PageRequest) -> String {\n            format!(\"ledger:v1:tenant={tenant}:page={}:size={}\", req.page, req.size)\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[async_trait]\n    impl<R> LedgerEntryService for LedgerEntryServiceImpl<R>\n    where\n        R: LedgerEntryRepository + Send + Sync,\n    {\n        async fn list_entries(\n            &self,\n            tenant: Uuid,\n            req: PageRequest,\n        ) -> Result<Paginated<super::LedgerEntryDto>, ApiError> {\n            let key = Self::make_cache_key(tenant, req);\n\n            // Use response cache (if redis unavailable, we            // Delegates to repository\n                        let (rows, total) = self.repo.fetch_page(tenant, req).await?;\n                        let meta = PageMeta {\n rule: amount cannot be 0.\n   256→        if entry.amount.is_zero() {\n   257→            return Err(LedgerError::InvalidState(\n   258→                \"Amount cannot be zero\".to_string(),\n   259→            ));\n   260→        }\n   261→\n   262→        let id = self.repo.insert(entry).await?;\n   263→\n   264→        // Invalidate parent list (simple strategy: delete entire key space).\n   265→        let mut conn = self.redis.get_async_connection().await?;\n   266→        let pattern = format!(\"tenant:{}:ledger_entry:*\", entry.tenant_id);\n   267→        let _: () = redis::cmd(\"UNLINK\").arg(pattern).query_async(&mut conn).await?;\n   268→\n   269→        // Evict in-memory cache.\n   270→        self.in_memory.write().await.pop(&id);\n   271→\n   272→        Ok(id)\n   273→    }\n   274→\n   275→    /// Fetches a single entry, going through L1 (memory) & L2 (Redis) caches.\n   276→    #[instrument(skip(self), level = \"debug\")]\n   277→    pub async fn get_entry(\n   278→        &self,\n   279→        tenant_id: i64,\n   280→        entry_id: i64,\n   281→    ) -> Result<LedgerEntryDto> {\n   282→        // 1. Check L1.\n   283→        if let Some(dto) = self.in_memory.read().await.get(&entry_id).cloned() {\n   284→            return Ok(dto);\n   285→        }\n   286→\n   287→        // 2. In a real code-base it would live in `src/ledger_entry.rs`.\n\nuse std::sync::Arc;\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse redis::AsyncCommands;\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse sqlx::{postgres::PgPoolOptions, PgPool, Row};\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::sync::RwLock;\nuse tracing::{instrument, Instrument};\n\n/// Max number of records the API will ever return in a single call.\n/// Acts as a safeguard against pathological requests.\nconst HARD_PAGE_SIZE_LIMIT: u16 = 1_000;\n\n/// Domain object representing an immutable entry in the ledger.\n///\n/// NOTE: The table uses `NUMERIC(38, 10)` for `amount` in Postgres but we\n/// materialise it as `rust_decimal::Decimal` to maintain precision.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct LedgerEntry {\n    pub id: i64,\n    pub tenant_id: i64,\n    pub account_id: i64,\n    pub amount: rust_decimal::Decimal,\n    pub currency: String,\n    pub booked_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub LedgerEntryDto {\n    pub uuid: String,\n    pub amount: rust_decimal::Decimal,\n    pub currency: String,\n    pub booked_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub memo: Option<String>,\n}\n\nimpl From<LedgerEntry> for LedgerEntryDto {\n    fn from(e: LedgerEntry) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            uuid: base62::encode(e.id as u128),\n            amount: e.amount,\n            currency: e.currency,\n            booked_at: e.booked_at,\n            memo: e.memo,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Pagination request coming from the API.\n///\n/// `page` is 1-based because it is more ergonomic for clients.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct PageRequest {\n    pub page: u32,\n    pub per_page: u16,\n}\n\nimpl PageRequest {\n    /// Sanitises caller input: caps `per_page` and guarantees `page >= 1`.\n    pub fn sanitise(self) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            page: self.page.max(1),\n            per_page: self.per_page.min(HARD_PAGE_SIZE_LIMIT).max(1),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Calculates SQL offset.\n    pub fn offset(&self) -> i64 {\n        ((self.page - 1) * self.per_page as u32) as i64\n    }\n}\n\n/// Structured page response returned to the API layer.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct Page<T> {\n    pub data: Vec<T>,\n    pub next_page: Option<u32>,\n    pub total: Option<u64>,\n}\n\n/// Shared error type for repository/service-level failures.\n#[derive(Error, Debug)]\npub enum LedgerError {\n    #[error(\"database error: {0}\")]\n    Db(#[from] sqlx::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"redis error: {0}\")]\n    Cache(#[from] redis::RedisError),\n\n    #[error(\"ledger entry not found\")]\n    NotFound,\n\n    #[error(\"invalid state: {0}\")]\n    InvalidState(String),\n}\n\npub type Result<T> = std::result::Result<T, LedgerError>;\n\n/// Abstraction hiding persistent storage semantics.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait LedgerEntryRepository: Send + })\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl LedgerEntryRepository for PostgresLedgerEntryRepo {\n    #[instrument(skip(self, entry), level = \"debug\")]\n    async fn insert(&self, entry: &LedgerEntry) -> VALUES  ($1,        $2,         $3,     $4,       $5,        $6)\n            RETURNING id;\n        \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(entry.tenant_id)\n        .bind(entry.account_id)\n        .bind(entry.amount)\n        .bind(&entry.currency)\n        .bind(entry.booked_at)\n        .bind(&entry.memo)\n        .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(rec.get::<i64, _>(\"id\"))\n    }\n\n    #[instrument(skip(self), level = \"trace\")]\n    async fn get(&self, tenant_id: i64, entry_id: i64) -> Result<LedgerEntry> {\n        let rec = sqlx::query_as!(\n            LedgerEntry,\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id, tenant_id, account_id, amount, currency, booked_at, memo\n            FROM   ledger_entries\n            WHERE  tenant_id = $1 AND id = $2\n        \"#,\n            tenant_id,\n            entry_id\n        )\n        .fetch_optional(&self.pool)\n        .await?\n        .ok_or(LedgerError::NotFound)?;\n\n        Ok(rec)\n    }\n\n    #[instrument(skip(self), level = \"debug\")]\n    async fn fetch_page(\n        &self,\n        tenant_id: i64,\n        req: PageRequest,\n    ) -> Result<(Vec<LedgerEntry>, u64)> {\n        // 1. Count total for the tenant.\n        let total: (i64,) = sqlx::query_as(\n            r#\"SELECT COUNT(*) as count FROM ledger_entries WHERE tenant_id = $1\"#,\n        )\n        .bind(tenant_id)\n        .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        // 2. Fetch requested slice.\n        let rows = sqlx::query_as!(\n            LedgerEntry,\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id, tenant_id, account_id, amount, currency, booked_at, memo\n            FROM   ledger_entries\n            WHERE  tenant_id = $1\n            ORDER  BY booked_at DESC, id DESC\n            OFFSET $2 LIMIT $3\n        \"#,\n            tenant_id,\n            req.offset(),\n            req.per_page as i64\n        )\n        .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok((rows, total.0 as u64))\n    }\n}\n\n/// High-level service orchestrating repository and distributed cache.\n///\n/// Caches individual `LedgerEntryDto` objects to minimise DB traffic for\n/// idempotent GET requests. The caching strategy is a simple “cache-aside”\n/// with a 10 minute TTL. We purposely keep the implementation thin; in real\n/// life we would centralise this logic through a reusable Redis wrapper.\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct LedgerEntryService<R: LedgerEntryRepository> {\n    repo: Arc<R>,\n    redis: redis::Client,\n    // Small in-memory cache to ameliorate thundering herd during short periods.\n    in_memory: Arc<RwLock<lru::LruCache<i64, LedgerEntryDto>>>,\n}\n\nimpl<R: LedgerEntryRepository> LedgerEntryService<R> {\n    pub fn new(repo: R, redis_url: &str, mem_cache_cap: usize) -> Result<Self> {\n        Ok(Self {\n            repo: Arc::new(repo),\n            redis: redis::Client::open(redis_url).map_err(LedgerError::Cache)?,\n            in_memory: Arc::new(RwLock::new(lru::LruCache::new(\n                mem_cache_cap.try_into().unwrap_or(256),\n            ))),\n        })\n    }\n\n    fn redis_key(tenant_id: i64, entry_id: i64) -> String {\n        format!(\"tenant:{tenant_id}:ledger_entry:{entry_id}\")\n    }\n\n    /// Inserts a new entry and invalidates affected caches.\n    #[instrument(skip(self, entry), level = \"info\")]\n    pub async fn create_entry(&self, entry: &LedgerEntry) -> Result<i64> {\n        // Basic validation rule: amount cannot be 0.\n        if entry.amount.is_zero() {\n            return Err(LedgerError::InvalidState(\n                \"Amount cannot be zero\".to_string(),\n            ));\n        }\n\n        let id = self.repo.insert(entry).await?;\n\n        // Invalidate parent list (simple strategy: delete entire key space).\n        let mut conn = self.redis.get_async_connection().await?;\n        let pattern = format!(\"tenant:{}:ledger_entry:*\", entry.tenant_id);\n        let _: () = redis::cmd(\"UNLINK\").arg(pattern).query_async(&mut conn).await?;\n\n        // Evict in-memory cache.\n        self.in_memory.write().await.pop(&id);\n\n        Ok(id)\n    }\n\n    /// Fetches a single entry, going through L1 (memory) & L2 (Redis) caches.\n    #[instrument(skip(self), level = \"debug\")]\n    pub async fn get_entry(\n        &self,\n        tenant_id: i64,\n        entry_id: i64,\n    ) -> Result<LedgerEntryDto> {\n        // 1. Check L1.\n        if let Some(dto) = self.in_memory.read().await.get(&entry_id).cloned() {\n            return Ok(dto);\n        }\n\n        // 2. Check Redis.\n        let redis_key = Self::redis_key(tenant_id, entry_id);\n        let mut conn = self.redis.get_async_connection().await?;\n        if let Ok(Some(binary)) = conn.get::<_, Vec<u8>>(&redis_key).await {\n            let dto: LedgerEntryDto = bincode::deserialize(&binary)\n                .map_err(|e| LedgerError::InvalidState(e.to_string()))?;\n            // Fill L1.\n            self.in_memory.write().await.put(entry_id, dto.clone());\n            return Ok(dto);\n        }\n\n        // 3. Fallback to DB.\n        let entry = self.repo.get(tenant_id, entry_id).await?;\n        let dto = LedgerEntryDto::from(entry.clone());\n\n        // Back-fill caches.\n        let _: () = conn\n            .set_ex(\n                &redis_key,\n                bincode::serialize(&dto).unwrap(),\n                600, // seconds\n            )\n            .await?;\n        self.in_memory.write().await.put(entry_id, dto.clone());\n\n        Ok(dto)\n    }\n\n    /// Retrieves a paginated list, with optional total count.\n    #[instrument(skip(self), level = \"debug\")]\n    pub async fn list_entries(\n        &self,\n        tenant_id: i64,\n        req: PageRequest,\n    ) -> Result<Page<LedgerEntryDto>> {\n        let req = req.sanitise();\n        // At scale we would paginated-cache “pages” too, but omit for brevity.\n        let (rows, total) = self.repo.fetch_page(tenant_id, req).await?;\n        let data: Vec<LedgerEntryDto> = rows.into_iter().map(Into::into).collect();\n\n        let consumed = (req.page as u64) * (req.per_page as u64);\n        let next_page = if consumed < total {\n            Some(req.page + 1)\n        } else {\n            None\n        };\n\n        Ok(Page {\n            data,\n            next_page,\n            total: Some(total),\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use rust_decimal_macros::dec;\n    use serial_test::serial;\n\n    // Helper to build an in-memory postgres (requires `cargo test -- --ignored`\n    // if dockerised). Kept minimal for brevity.\n    async fn bootstrap_repo() -> PostgresLedgerEntryRepo {\n        let db_url =\n            std::env::var(\"TEST_DATABASE_URL\").expect(\"Set TEST_DATABASE_URL\");\n        let repo = PostgresLedgerEntryRepo::new(&db_url, 5)\n            .await\n            .expect(\"failed to init repo\");\n\n        // Initialise schema (idempotent).\n        sqlx::migrate!().run(&repo.pool).await.unwrap();\n\n        repo\n    }\n\n    #[tokio::test]\n    #[serial] // Avoid test inter-dependency on the DB.\n    async fn crud_roundtrip() {\n        let repo = bootstrap_repo().await;\n        let svc =\n            LedgerEntryService::new(repo, \"redis://127.0.0.1/\", 128).unwrap();\n\n        let entry = LedgerEntry {\n            id: 0,\n            tenant_id: 42,\n            account_id: 100,\n            amount: dec!(123.45),\n            currency: \"USD\".into(),\n            booked_at: Utc::now(),\n            memo: Some(\"integration test\".into()),\n        };\n\n        let id = svc.create_entry(&entry).await.unwrap();\n        assert!(id > 0);\n\n        let dto1 = svc.get_entry(42, id).await.unwrap();\n        let dto2 = svc.get_entry(42, id).await.unwrap(); // should hit cache\n\n        assert_eq!(dto1.amount, dto2.amount);\n\n        let page = svc\n            .list_entries(\n                42,\n                PageRequest {\n                    page: 1,\n                    per_page: 10,\n                },\n            )\n            .await\n            .unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(page.data.len(), 1);\n    }\n}\n```","numLines":401,"startLine":1,"totalLines":401}}}

In production, replace with `redis::aio::ConnectionLike` et al.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait Cache: Send + Sync {\n    async fn get_bytes(&self, key: &str) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, LedgerError>;\n    async fn set_bytes(&self, key: &str, value: &[u8], ttl: Duration) -> Result<(), LedgerError>;\n}\n\n/// Redis implementation.\npub struct RedisCache {\n    client: redis::Client,\n}\n\nimpl RedisCache      Ok(Self { client })\n    }\n\n    async fn conn(&self) -> Result<redis::aio::Connection, LedgerError> {\n        self.client\n            .get_async_connection()\n            .await\n            .map_err(|e| LedgerError::cache(e.to_string()))\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl Cache for RedisCache {\n    async fn get_bytes(&self, key: &str) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, LedgerError> {\n /// High-level query function to be consumed by HTTP/GraphQL handlers.\n    pub async fn list_entries(\n        &self,\n        filter: LedgerEntryFilter,\n        pagination: Pagination,\n    ) -> Result<LedgerEntryPage, LedgerError> {\n        // Step 1: validate user input\n        filter.validate()?;\n        pagination.validate()?;\n\n        // Step 2: cache lookup\n        let cache_key = Self::make_cache_key(&filter, &pagination);\n        if let Some(bytes) = self.cache.get_bytes(&cache_key).await? {\n            let page: LedgerEntryPage = serde_json::from_slice(&bytes)?;\n ((total_items as f64) / (pagination.per_page as f64)).ceil() as u32;\n\n        let page = LedgerEntryPage {\n            items,\n            total_items,\n            total_pages,\n            page: = serde_json::to_vec(&page) {\n            let _ = self\n                .cache\n                .set_bytes(&cache_key, &bytes, CACHE_TTL)\n                .await;\n        }\n\n        Ok(page)\n sqlx::Encode<'q, Postgres> + sqlx::Type<Postgres> + Send + Sync)>,\n    {\n        for b in binds {\n            self = self.bind(b);\n        }\n        self\n    }\n}\n\n/* Err(ValidationError::new(\"limit_out_of_bounds\"));\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\nimpl Pagination {\n    /// Tries to build a [`Pagination`] struct from an arbitrary query map,\n    /// returning validation errors early.\n    pub fn try_from_query_map(\n        params: &HashMap<String, String>,\n    ) -> Result<Self, PaginationError> {\n        let offset = params\n            .get(\"offset\")\n            .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u32>().ok())\n            .unwrap_or_default();\n\n        let limit = params\n            .get(\"limit\")\n            .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u32>().ok())\n            .unwrap_or_else(|| Self::default().limit);\n\n        let sort = params.get(\"sort\").cloned();\n\n        let candidate = Self { offset, limit, sort };\n\n        candidate.validate()?;\n\n        Ok(candidate)\n    }\n\n    /// Calculates SQL LIMIT/OFFSET for the current page.\n    #[must_use]\n    pub fn sql_limit_offset(&self) -> (u32, u32) {\n        (self.limit, self.offset)\n    }\n}\n\n/// The envelope returned by list-style endpoints.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize)]\npub struct Paginated<T>\nwhere\n    T: Serialize,\n{\n    pub items:  ) -> Self {\n        let has_more = (pagination.offset as u64 + items.len() as u64) < total;\n        let cache_hint = cacheable.then(|| CacheHint::new(DEFAULT_TTL_SECONDS));\n\n        Self {\n            items,\n            total,\n            offset:   pagination.offset,\n            limit:    pagination.limit,\n            has_more,\n            cache_hint,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// A *soft* {\n    #[error(\"validation failure: {0}\")]\n    Validation(#[from] validator::ValidationErrors),\n    #[error(\"not found\")]\n    NotFound,\n    #[error(\"database error: {0}\")]\n    Sqlx(#[from]                 */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Row mapping for the `ledger_entries` table. DateTime<Utc>,\n}\n\n/// Strongly-typed wrapper over *sqlx* PgPool implementing the\n/// [`PaginatedRepository`] trait for [`LedgerEntry`].\npub struct LedgerEntryPgRepository {\n    pool: }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl PaginatedRepository for LedgerEntryPgRepository {\n    type Output = LedgerEntry;\n\n    #[instrument(skip_all, name = \"repo.ledger.list\")]\n    async fn paginated_list(\n        &self,\n        pagination: &Pagination,\n    ) -> Result<Paginated<Self::Output>, PaginationError> {\n        // -------------------- Build the dynamic SQL query --------------------\n        // We expect an index on ( account_id, created_at DESC ) in production.\n        let mut builder: QueryBuilder<Postgres> =\n            QueryBuilder::new(\"SELECT * FROM ledger_entries \");\n\n        // Optional ORDER BY clause from the client — only allow whitelisted cols.\n        if let Some(sort_spec) = &pagination.sort {\n            match sort_spec.as_str() {\n                \"created_at:asc\"  => builder.push(\"ORDER BY created_at ASC \"),\n                \"created_at:desc\" => builder.push(\"ORDER BY created_at DESC \"),\n                \"amount:asc\"      => builder.push(\"ORDER BY amount ASC \"),\n                \"amount:desc\"     => builder.push(\"ORDER BY amount DESC \"),\n                _ => {\n                    return Err(PaginationError::Validation(\n                        ValidationError::new(\"invalid_sort\").into(),\n                    ));\n                }\n            }\n        } else {\n entries: Vec<LedgerEntry> = builder\n            .build_query_as()\n            .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n            .await?;\n\n        // -------------------- Get the *total* record count -------------------\n        let total: (i64,) =\n            sqlx::query_as(\"SELECT COUNT(*) as count FROM ledger_entries\")\n                .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n                .await?;\n\n /*cacheable=*/ true,\n        ))\n    }\n}\n\n/// An *idempotent* command that inserts a ledger entry inside its own\n/// transaction. Ledger Entry Query Service with pagination, request-level validation and\n//! Redis-backed response caching.  This module deliberately focuses on the\n//! “Query” half of CQRS, while remaining agnostic of the web framework that\n//! ultimately wires it up to REST/GraphQL routes.\n//!\n//! The code is intended to be production–ready: it embraces asynchronous I/O,\n//! proper error handling, structured logging (via `tracing`) and defensive\n//! validation.  It also demonstrates the Repository pattern, exposing a domain\n//! interface and hiding persistence details (PostgreSQL in our case).\n//!\n//! NOTE: Some crate features (e.g. `sqlx` macros) require the corresponding\n//! Cargo.toml declarations elsewhere in the project:\n//\n//! ```toml\n//! [dependencies]\n//! async-trait = \"0.1\"\n//! chrono       = { version = \"0.4\", features = [\"serde\"] }\n//! deadpool-redis = { version = \"0.13\", features = [\"tokio1\"] }\n//! redis        = { # Overview\n//!\n//! • `LedgerEntry`: Domain Model  \n//! • `LedgerEntryView`: DTO for external representation  \n//! • `Pagination`: Helper for page/size bounds + validation  \n//! • forward-compatibility via versioned fields.\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct LedgerEntryView {\n    pub id:           Uuid,\n    pub account_id:   Uuid,\n    pub amount:       String, // e.g., `\"123.45 USD\"`\n    pub direction:    EntryType,\n    pub created_at:   DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub description:  Option<String>,\n}\n\nimpl From<LedgerEntry> for LedgerEntryView {\n    fn from(src: LedgerEntry) -> Self {\n        let sign = if src.entry_type == EntryType::Debit { \"-\" } else { \"\" };\n        // Assume 2 decimal places for simplicity; a production system would\n        // leverage `rust_decimal` or similar.\n        let amount_major = (src.amount_minor as f64) / 100.0;\n        LedgerEntryView {\n            id:          src.id,\n            account_id:  src.account_id,\n            amount:      format!(\"{}{:.2} {}\", sign, amount_major.abs(), src.currency),\n            direction:   src.entry_type,\n            created_at:  src.created_at,\n            description: src.description,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Pagination\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nconst MAX_PER_PAGE: u32 = 250;\n\n/// Strongly-typed pagination request (pre-validated).\n#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]\npub struct Pagination {\n    pub page:     u32,\n    pub per_page: u32,\n}\n\nimpl Pagination {\n    /// Validate and create a Pagination instance.\n    pub fn new(page: u32, per_page: u32) -> Result<Self, LedgerError> {\n        if page == 0 {\n            return Err(LedgerError::validation(\"`page` must be >= 1\"));\n        }\n        if per_page == 0 || per_page > MAX_PER_PAGE {\n            return Err(LedgerError::validation(&format!(\n                \"`per_page` must be in 1..={MAX_PER_PAGE}\"\n            )));\n        }\n        Ok(Self { page, per_page })\n    }\n\n    pub fn offset_limit(self) -> (i64, i64) {\n        (\n            ((self.page - 1) * self.per_page) as i64,\n            self.per_page as i64,\n        )\n    }\n}\n\n/// Server response envelope for paginated lists.\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]\npub struct Paginated<T> {\n    pub data:       Vec<T>,\n    pub total:      u64,\n    pub page:       u32,\n    pub per_page:   u32,\n}\n\n// #[instrument(skip(self))]\n    async fn fetch_by_account(\n        &self,\n        account_id: Uuid,\n        pagination: Pagination,\n    ) -> Result<(Vec<LedgerEntry>, u64), LedgerError> {\n account_id, amount_minor, currency, entry_type as \"entry_type: EntryType\",\n                   created_at, description\n              FROM ledger_entries\n             WHERE account_id = $1\n             ORDER BY created_at DESC\n             OFFSET $2 LIMIT $3\n            \"#,\n            account_id,\n            offset,\n            limit\n        )\n        .map(|row| LedgerEntry {\n  R: LedgerEntryRepository + 'static,\n    {\n        Self {\n            repo: Arc::new(repo),\n            cache,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Fetch paginated ledger entries with Redis caching.\n    ///\n    /// Cache key is constructed from `account_id/page/per_page`.\n    #[instrument(skip(self))]\n    pub async fn list_entries(\n        &self,\n        account_id: Uuid,\n        pagination: Try cache\n        let mut redis_con = self.cache.get().await?;\n        if let Ok(Some(cached_json)) = redis_con.get::<_, String>(&cache_key).await {\n            debug!(%cache_key, \"Cache hit for ledger entries\");\n            let paginated: Paginated<LedgerEntryView> = serde_json::from_str(&cached_json)\n                .map_err(|e| LedgerError::Internal(e.to_string()))?;\n            return Ok(paginated);\n        }\n        debug!(%cache_key, \"Cache miss for ledger entries\");\n\n        // Step 2. Fire-and-forget cache population\n        let cache_payload = serde_json::to_string(&result)\n            .map_err(|e| LedgerError::Internal(e.to_string()))?;\n        let mut redis_con_clone = usize)\n                .await;\n        });\n\n        Ok(result)\n    }\n}\n\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Unit Tests\n// Contains an in-process read/write lock guard around the\n/// connection to avoid stampeding‐herd scenarios in high load.\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct ResponseCache {\n    conn: Arc<RwLock<ConnectionManager>>,\n    prefix: String,\n}\n\nimpl ResponseCache {\n    /// Create a new instance from an existing Redis connection.\n    pub fn new(prefix: impl Into<String>, conn: ConnectionManager) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            conn: Arc::new(RwLock::new(conn)),\n            prefix: prefix.into(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Assemble a namespaced key for Redis.  Uses BLAKE3 on the raw bytes to\n    /// generate a short, collision-resistant digest.\n    fn build_key(&self, namespace: &str, payload: &[u8]) -> String {\n        let mut h = Blake3::new();\n        h.update(payload);\n        let digest = h.finalize();\n        format!(\"{}:{}:{}\", self.prefix, namespace, digest.to_hex())\n    }\n\n    /// Serialize & store an item in Redis with the provided policy.\n    #[instrument(skip(self, value), fields(namespace = %ns, key = %key))]\n    async fn write<T>(&self, ns: &str, key: &str, value: &T, policy: CachePolicy) -> Result<(), CacheError>\n    where\n        T: Serialize + Send + Sync,\n    {\n        let payload = serde_json::to_vec(value)?;\n        let mut conn = self.conn.write().await;\n\n        debug!(\"caching response – ttl={}s, bytes={}\", policy.ttl.as_secs(), payload.len());\n        conn.set_ex::<&str, Vec<u8>, ()>(key, payload, policy.ttl.as_secs() as usize)\n            .await?;\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    /// Attempt to read & deserialize a value; returns Ok(None) on cache-miss.\n    #[instrument(skip(self))]\n    async fn read<T>(&self, key: &str) -> Result<Option<T>, CacheError>\n    where\n        T: DeserializeOwned,\n    {\n        let mut conn = self.conn.write().await;\n\n        let raw: Option<Vec<u8>> = conn.get(key).await?;\n        if let Some(blob) = raw {\n            trace!(\"cache hit – key={}, bytes={}\", key, blob.len());\n            let deserialized = serde_json::from_slice::<T>(&blob)?;\n            Ok(Some(deserialized))\n        } else {\n            trace!(\"cache miss – key={}\", key);\n            Ok(None)\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Public helper to retrieve a cached entry or compute & populate it.\n    ///\n    ///     let result: MyDto = cache.get_or_resolve(&query).await?;\n    ///\n    #[instrument(skip(self, query))]\n    pub async fn get_or_resolve<Q>(&self, query: &Q) -> Result<Q, CacheError>\n    where\n        Q: Cacheable,\n    {\n        let ns = query.namespace();\n        let payload = serde_json::to_vec(query)?;\n        let key = self.build_key(&ns, &payload);\n\n        // 1. Attempt to read\n        if let Some(res) = self.read::<Q>(&key).await? {\n            return Ok(res);\n        }\n\n        // 2. Miss → resolve & fill cache\n        let resolved = query.resolve().await?;\n        self.write(&ns, &key, &resolved, query.policy()).await?;\n\n        Ok(resolved)\n    }\n\n    /// Remove all keys for a given tenant namespace.  Useful when a ledger\n    /// mutation happens and cached projections must be invalidated.\n    #[instrument(skip(self))]\n    pub async fn purge_namespace(&self, namespace: &str) -> Result<u64, CacheError> {\n        let pattern = format!(\"{}:{}:*\", self.prefix, namespace);\n        let mut conn = self.conn.write().await;\n        // NOTE: SCAN could be more graceful for prod, kept simple here.\n        let keys: Vec<String> = conn.keys(pattern).await?;\n        let deleted: u64 = conn.del(keys).await?;\n        debug!(\"purged {} stale cache entries under '{}'\", deleted, namespace);\n        Ok(deleted)\n    }\n}\n\n// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Sample DTO + Cacheable impl for demonstration\n// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct LedgerSnapshotQuery {\n    /// Tenant/organisation id\n uuid::Uuid,\n    pub as_of: chrono::NaiveDate,\n    pub balance: rust_decimal::Decimal,\n    pub generated_at: u64, // unix-epoch (ms)\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl Cacheable for LedgerSnapshot {\n    fn namespace(&self) -> String {\n        format!(\"tenant:{}:v1\", self.tenant_id)\n    }\n\n    fn policy(&self) -> CachePolicy {\n        CachePolicy::MEDIUM\n    }\n\n    /// Heavy-weight computation }\n}\n\n// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Unit tests (cargo test -p api_microservice -- src/module_9.txt)\n// fields(tenant = %auth.tenant_id))]\npub async fn get_ledger_entries(\n    auth: AuthContext,\n    Query(dto): Query<LedgerEntryQueryDto>,\n    state: Data<AppState>,\n) -> Result<Json<Paginated<LedgerEntry>>, ApiError> {\n    // ... validation ...\n\n    let cache_key = make_cache_key(&auth.tenant_id, &dto);\n\n    // Attempt cache hit\n    if let Ok(cached) = redis_conn.get::<_, Vec<u8>>(cache_key.clone()).await {\n        if !cached.is_empty() {\n            debug!(\"cache hit\");\n            let value: Paginated<LedgerEntry> = 197-209\n\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]\npub struct ResponseCacheConfig {\n    #[serde(default)]\n    pub enabled: bool,\n    #[serde(default = \"default_cache_duration\")]\n    pub duration: Analysis\n\n### Architectural Overview\n\nThe LedgerLink Nexus microservice follows a CQRS (Command Query Responsibility Segregation) inspired architecture with:\n\n1. **View Models (DTOs)**: `LedgerEntryDto`, `LedgerEntryView`, `Paginated<T>`\n2. **Domain Models**: `LedgerEntry`, `AccountSnapshot`\n3. **Repository Layer**: Abstractions like `LedgerEntryRepository` with PostgreSQL implementations\n4. **Service Layer**: Query services that orchestrate caching and repository access\n5. **Response Caching**: Redis-backed caching with configurable TTLs (ranging from 15 seconds to 5 minutes)\n\n### Component Interactions\n\nThe **read path** flows as follows:\n1. HTTP Handler (e.g., `get_ledger_entries` in module_26) receives request\n2. Cache key is generated from tenant ID + query parameters\n3. Redis cache is checked first (`cache.get()`)\n4. On cache miss, the repository fetches from PostgreSQL\n5. Response is serialized and stored in Redis with TTL (`set_ex`)\n\nThe **write path** flows as follows:\n1. Write handler/service receives mutation request\n2. Database INSERT/UPDATE is executed within a transaction\n3. **No cache invalidation occurs** - this is the flaw\n\n### Root Cause of Stale Data Issue\n\n**The fundamental architectural flaw is the absence of an invalidation signal from write handlers to the cache layer.**\n\nSpecifically:\n- Read handlers in `module_44`, `module_26`, `module_52`, `module_50`, and `module_55` implement read-through caching with Redis\n- Write handlers in `module_38` execute database INSERTs but do **not** call any cache invalidation method\n- The caching module (`module_9`) has a `purge_namespace()` method designed exactly for this purpose, but it is never invoked by write operations\n- The cache entries persist until their TTL expires (60 seconds to 5 minutes depending on configuration)\n\nThis creates a window where:\n1. Client A reads ledger entries → cached\n2. Client B updates a ledger entry → DB updated, cache NOT invalidated\n3. Client A reads ledger entries again → receives stale cached data\n4. Only after TTL expiration does Client A see the updated data\n\n### Comparison with module_67\n\nInterestingly, `module_67` shows a partial attempt at cache invalidation in `create_entry()`:\n\n```rust\n// module_67.txt lines 264-270\n// Invalidate parent list (simple strategy: delete entire key space).\nlet mut conn = self.redis.get_async_connection().await?;\nlet pattern = format!(\"tenant:{}:ledger_entry:*\", entry.tenant_id);\nlet _: () = redis::cmd(\"UNLINK\").arg(pattern).query_async(&mut conn).await?;\n```\n\nHowever, this invalidates **individual entry caches** (key pattern `tenant:{id}:ledger_entry:*`) but NOT the **paginated list caches** which use different key patterns like:\n- `ledger_entries:{tenant}:{filter}:{page}:{per_page}` (module_44)\n- `ledger:v1:tenant={tenant}:page={page}:size={size}` (module_72)\n- `ledger_entries:v1:{tenant}:{page}:{per_page}:...` (module_26)\n\n## Implementation\n\n### Proposed Solution: Explicit Cache Invalidation Pattern\n\nThe recommended architectural solution is **explicit cache invalidation** (also known as cache-aside with write-through invalidation).\n\n**Design Pattern**: After each successful database write operation, the write handler must explicitly signal the cache layer to invalidate all affected cache entries.\n\n**Justification for this approach over alternatives**:\n\n1. **Why not just lower the cache TTL?**\n   - Reducing TTL (e.g., to 1 second) would severely impact performance and defeat the purpose of caching\n   - Still creates a consistency window, just smaller\n   - Does not scale - you cannot tune TTL per mutation type\n\n2. **Why not write-through caching?**\n   - Write-through would require updating all possible cache keys on every write\n   - Pagination makes this impractical (would need to update N page variations)\n   - More complex to implement correctly\n\n3. **Why explicit invalidation is superior**:\n   - Immediate consistency after mutations\n   - Targeted - only invalidates relevant cache entries\n   - Maintains high cache hit rates for unchanged data\n   - Aligns with existing `purge_namespace()` infrastructure\n\n### Modules Requiring Modification\n\n1. **Write Handler Module (module_38.txt)**\n   - The `insert()` method needs to accept a cache reference and call invalidation\n\n2. **Caching Service Module (module_9.txt or module_72.txt)**\n   - Already has `purge_namespace()` and `purge()` methods - these need to be exposed/called\n\n3. **Service Layer Modules (module_44.txt, module_52.txt, module_50.txt)**\n   - Write operations need to coordinate with the response cache\n\n### New Interactions Required\n\n```\n┌─────────────────┐         ┌──────────────────┐         ┌─────────────┐\n│  Write Handler  │────────▶│  Database Layer  │────────▶│  PostgreSQL │\n│  (module_38)    │         │  (Repository)    │         │             │\n└────────┬────────┘         └──────────────────┘         └─────────────┘\n         │\n         │ On Success\n         ▼\n┌─────────────────┐         ┌──────────────────┐\n│  Cache Service  │────────▶│     Redis        │\n│  (module_9)     │ DELETE  │                  │\n│  invalidate()   │ keys    │                  │\n└─────────────────┘         └──────────────────┘\n```\n\n### Implementation Outline\n\n1. **Modify write handlers to inject cache service**:\n```rust\n// In module_38.txt - add cache dependency\npub struct LedgerEntryPgRepository {\n    pool: PgPool,\n    cache: ResponseCache,  // NEW: inject cache service\n}\n\nimpl LedgerEntryPgRepository {\n    pub async fn insert(&self, entry: &LedgerEntry) -> Result<(), PaginationError> {\n        // ... existing INSERT logic ...\n\n        tx.commit().await?;\n\n        // NEW: Invalidate paginated response cache for this tenant/account\n        let namespace = format!(\"ledger_entries:{}\", entry.account_id);\n        self.cache.purge_namespace(&namespace).await?;\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n```\n\n2. **Ensure cache key patterns are consistent**:\n   - Read handlers and invalidation logic must use matching key patterns\n   - Consider using a shared `CacheKey` builder to ensure consistency\n\n3. **Add invalidation to all write paths**:\n   - INSERT operations\n   - UPDATE operations\n   - DELETE operations\n   - Bulk/batch operations\n\n## Summary\n\n- **Primary finding**: The stale data issue is caused by the **absence of cache invalidation signals** from write handlers to the Redis response cache. Write operations modify the PostgreSQL database but do not notify the caching layer, leaving cached responses stale until TTL expiration.\n\n- **Key components**:\n  - **Caching Module**: `module_9.txt` (core ResponseCache), `module_44.txt`, `module_72.txt`\n  - **Read Handlers**: `module_26.txt` (actix-web GET endpoint), `module_52.txt`, `module_44.txt` (Axum)\n  - **Write Handlers**: `module_38.txt` (insert without invalidation)\n  - **Configuration**: `config.txt` (ResponseCacheConfig with 5-minute default TTL)\n\n- **Architectural pattern**: The system uses a **cache-aside (lazy loading)** pattern for reads but lacks the corresponding **cache invalidation** on writes. The fix requires implementing **explicit cache invalidation** by having write handlers call the existing `purge_namespace()` or `purge()` methods in the cache service after successful database mutations.\n\n- **Recommendations**:\n  1. Modify `module_38.txt` write handlers to call cache invalidation after DB commits\n  2. Ensure consistent cache key generation between read and write paths\n  3. Use the existing `purge_namespace()` method in `module_9.txt` for targeted invalidation\n  4. Consider adding a dedicated `CacheInvalidator` service to centralize invalidation logic\n  5. Add tracing/metrics to track cache invalidation events for observability\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":455,"cache_read_input_tokens":101222,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":455,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18,"service_tier":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"16fc3ebe-afa8-4a83-8d80-78e2e027f973","uuid":"b5d48dfe-8728-456e-be00-0950fc2d7fc7"}

NOTHING\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(entry.id)\n        .bind(entry.account_id)\n        .bind(entry.amount)\n        .bind(&entry.currency)\n        .bind(&entry.description)\n        .bind(entry.created_at)\n        .execute(&mut tx)\n        .await?;\n\n        tx.commit().await?;\n        Ok(())\n        // NOTE: NO CACHE INVALIDATION IS PERFORMED HERE\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Read Handler with Response Caching (module_26.txt)\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_26.txt\n// Lines: 317-386\n\n#[get(\"/api/v1/ledger/entries\")]\n#[instrument(skip(dto, state, auth), fields(tenant = %auth.tenant_id))]\npub async fn get_ledger_entries(\n    auth: AuthContext,\n    Query(dto): Query<LedgerEntryQueryDto>,\n    state: Data<AppState>,\n) -> Result<Json<Paginated<LedgerEntry>>, ApiError> {\n    // ... validation ...\n\n    let cache_key = make_cache_key(&auth.tenant_id, &dto);\n\n    // Attempt cache hit\n    if let Ok(cached) = redis_conn.get::<_, Vec<u8>>(cache_key.clone()).await {\n        if !cached.is_empty() {\n            debug!(\"cache hit\");\n            let value: Paginated<LedgerEntry> = serde_json::from_slice(&cached)?;\n            return Ok(Json(value));\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Cache miss – fetching from service layer\n    let result = state.query_service.query_entries(filter, pagination).await?;\n\n    // Store in cache – set TTL 60s\n    let ttl_seconds = 60;\n    if let Ok(serialized) = serde_json::to_vec(&result) {\n        let _: () = redis_conn.set_ex(cache_key, serialized, ttl_seconds).await?;\n    }\n\n    Ok(Json(result))\n}\n```\n\n### Response Cache Configuration (config.txt)\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/config.txt\n// Lines: 197-209\n\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]\npub struct ResponseCacheConfig {\n    #[serde(default)]\n    pub enabled: bool,\n    #[serde(default = \"default_cache_duration\")]\n    pub duration: Duration,\n    #[serde(default)]\n    pub respect_cache_control_header: bool,\n}\n\nfn default_cache_duration() -> Duration {\n    Duration::from_secs(5 * 60) // 5 minutes\n}\n```\n\n## Analysis\n\n### Architectural Overview\n\nThe LedgerLink Nexus microservice follows a CQRS (Command Query Responsibility Segregation) inspired architecture with:\n\n1. **View Models (DTOs)**: `LedgerEntryDto`, `LedgerEntryView`, `Paginated<T>`\n2. **Domain Models**: `LedgerEntry`, `AccountSnapshot`\n3. **Repository Layer**: Abstractions like `LedgerEntryRepository` with PostgreSQL implementations\n4. **Service Layer**: Query services that orchestrate caching and repository access\n5. **Response Caching**: Redis-backed caching with configurable TTLs (ranging from 15 seconds to 5 minutes)\n\n### Component Interactions\n\nThe **read path** flows as follows:\n1. HTTP Handler (e.g., `get_ledger_entries` in module_26) receives request\n2. Cache key is generated from tenant ID + query parameters\n3. Redis cache is checked first (`cache.get()`)\n4. On cache miss, the repository fetches from PostgreSQL\n5. Response is serialized and stored in Redis with TTL (`set_ex`)\n\nThe **write path** flows as follows:\n1. Write handler/service receives mutation request\n2. Database INSERT/UPDATE is executed within a transaction\n3. **No cache invalidation occurs** - this is the flaw\n\n### Root Cause of Stale Data Issue\n\n**The fundamental architectural flaw is the absence of an invalidation signal from write handlers to the cache layer.**\n\nSpecifically:\n- Read handlers in `module_44`, `module_26`, `module_52`, `module_50`, and `module_55` implement read-through caching with Redis\n- Write handlers in `module_38` execute database INSERTs but do **not** call any cache invalidation method\n- The caching module (`module_9`) has a `purge_namespace()` method designed exactly for this purpose, but it is never invoked by write operations\n- The cache entries persist until their TTL expires (60 seconds to 5 minutes depending on configuration)\n\nThis creates a window where:\n1. Client A reads ledger entries → cached\n2. Client B updates a ledger entry → DB updated, cache NOT invalidated\n3. Client A reads ledger entries again → receives stale cached data\n4. Only after TTL expiration does Client A see the updated data\n\n### Comparison with module_67\n\nInterestingly, `module_67` shows a partial attempt at cache invalidation in `create_entry()`:\n\n```rust\n// module_67.txt lines 264-270\n// Invalidate parent list (simple strategy: delete entire key space).\nlet mut conn = self.redis.get_async_connection().await?;\nlet pattern = format!(\"tenant:{}:ledger_entry:*\", entry.tenant_id);\nlet _: () = redis::cmd(\"UNLINK\").arg(pattern).query_async(&mut conn).await?;\n```\n\nHowever, this invalidates **individual entry caches** (key pattern `tenant:{id}:ledger_entry:*`) but NOT the **paginated list caches** which use different key patterns like:\n- `ledger_entries:{tenant}:{filter}:{page}:{per_page}` (module_44)\n- `ledger:v1:tenant={tenant}:page={page}:size={size}` (module_72)\n- `ledger_entries:v1:{tenant}:{page}:{per_page}:...` (module_26)\n\n## Implementation\n\n### Proposed Solution: Explicit Cache Invalidation Pattern\n\nThe recommended architectural solution is **explicit cache invalidation** (also known as cache-aside with write-through invalidation).\n\n**Design Pattern**: After each successful database write operation, the write handler must explicitly signal the cache layer to invalidate all affected cache entries.\n\n**Justification for this approach over alternatives**:\n\n1. **Why not just lower the cache TTL?**\n   - Reducing TTL (e.g., to 1 second) would severely impact performance and defeat the purpose of caching\n   - Still creates a consistency window, just smaller\n   - Does not scale - you cannot tune TTL per mutation type\n\n2. **Why not write-through caching?**\n   - Write-through would require updating all possible cache keys on every write\n   - Pagination makes this impractical (would need to update N page variations)\n   - More complex to implement correctly\n\n3. **Why explicit invalidation is superior**:\n   - Immediate consistency after mutations\n   - Targeted - only invalidates relevant cache entries\n   - Maintains high cache hit rates for unchanged data\n   - Aligns with existing `purge_namespace()` infrastructure\n\n### Modules Requiring Modification\n\n1. **Write Handler Module (module_38.txt)**\n   - The `insert()` method needs to accept a cache reference and call invalidation\n\n2. **Caching Service Module (module_9.txt or module_72.txt)**\n   - Already has `purge_namespace()` and `purge()` methods - these need to be exposed/called\n\n3. **Service Layer Modules (module_44.txt, module_52.txt, module_50.txt)**\n   - Write operations need to coordinate with the response cache\n\n### New Interactions Required\n\n```\n┌─────────────────┐         ┌──────────────────┐         ┌─────────────┐\n│  Write Handler  │────────▶│  Database Layer  │────────▶│  PostgreSQL │\n│  (module_38)    │         │  (Repository)    │         │             │\n└────────┬────────┘         └──────────────────┘         └─────────────┘\n         │\n         │ On Success\n         ▼\n┌─────────────────┐         ┌──────────────────┐\n│  Cache Service  │────────▶│     Redis        │\n│  (module_9)     │ DELETE  │                  │\n│  invalidate()   │ keys    │                  │\n└─────────────────┘         └──────────────────┘\n```\n\n### Implementation Outline\n\n1. **Modify write handlers to inject cache service**:\n```rust\n// In module_38.txt - add cache dependency\npub struct LedgerEntryPgRepository {\n    pool: PgPool,\n    cache: ResponseCache,  // NEW: inject cache service\n}\n\nimpl LedgerEntryPgRepository {\n    pub async fn insert(&self, entry: &LedgerEntry) -> Result<(), PaginationError> {\n        // ... existing INSERT logic ...\n\n        tx.commit().await?;\n\n        // NEW: Invalidate paginated response cache for this tenant/account\n        let namespace = format!(\"ledger_entries:{}\", entry.account_id);\n        self.cache.purge_namespace(&namespace).await?;\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n```\n\n2. **Ensure cache key patterns are consistent**:\n   - Read handlers and invalidation logic must use matching key patterns\n   - Consider using a shared `CacheKey` builder to ensure consistency\n\n3. **Add invalidation to all write paths**:\n   - INSERT operations\n   - UPDATE operations\n   - DELETE operations\n   - Bulk/batch operations\n\n## Summary\n\n- **Primary finding**: The stale data issue is caused by the **absence of cache invalidation signals** from write handlers to the Redis response cache. Write operations modify the PostgreSQL database but do not notify the caching layer, leaving cached responses stale until TTL expiration.\n\n- **Key components**:\n  - **Caching Module**: `module_9.txt` (core ResponseCache), `module_44.txt`, `module_72.txt`\n  - **Read Handlers**: `module_26.txt` (actix-web GET endpoint), `module_52.txt`, `module_44.txt` (Axum)\n  - **Write Handlers**: `module_38.txt` (insert without invalidation)\n  - **Configuration**: `config.txt` (ResponseCacheConfig with 5-minute default TTL)\n\n- **Architectural pattern**: The system uses a **cache-aside (lazy loading)** pattern for reads but lacks the corresponding **cache invalidation** on writes. The fix requires implementing **explicit cache invalidation** by having write handlers call the existing `purge_namespace()` or `purge()` methods in the cache service after successful database mutations.\n\n- **Recommendations**:\n  1. Modify `module_38.txt` write handlers to call cache invalidation after DB commits\n  2. Ensure consistent cache key generation between read and write paths\n  3. Use the existing `purge_namespace()` method in `module_9.txt` for targeted invalidation\n  4. Consider adding a dedicated `CacheInvalidator` service to centralize invalidation logic\n  5. Add tracing/metrics to track cache invalidation events for observability\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
